PROMPT   : regarding 1.1 Dermal_sens.pdf, answer the following: Determine the
           exposure type (ORAL, DERMAL, or INHALATION) for the toxicology
           study, then classify the exposure method.
EXPECTED : DERMAL : Intradermal Injection and Occlusive Patch
ANSWER   : The toxicology study described in the document is for **dermal**
           exposure [2].

           The exposure method used was the **Magnusson and Kligman
           maximization test** [2]. This method involved the following
           procedures:

           * **Intradermal Induction:** The test material was administered at
           a concentration of 1% w/v in distilled water [6].
           * **Topical Induction:** The test material was administered at a
           concentration of 50% w/w in distilled water [6].
           * **Topical Challenge:** The test material was administered at
           concentrations of 25% and 10% w/w in distilled water [8].

[Q 11/11]  chat 941  1.8s
PROMPT   : regarding 1.3_Oral_repd, answer the following: Answer this question
           only if the study is a repeated dose study. Otherwise, answer 'not
           applicable'. What effects on the test animals changed the NOAEL or
           the classification hazard? This could be changes in food
           consumption, organ weight change, weight change, or any other
           health problem observed in the animal due to the substance. If
           there were none, answer "null".
EXPECTED : APPROPRIATE FOOD CONSUMPTION : Significant reduction in appetite
           <br>BODY WEIGHT CHANGE : Decrease in body weight gain, with
           affected rats reaching approximately half the body weight of
           control animals by study's end <br>ORGAN WEIGHT CHANGES: Changes in
           liver and kidney weights compared to controls <br>HEALTH PROBLEMS
           OBSERVED: Histopathological changes observed specifically in testis
ANSWER   : The study is a repeated dose study, as it involved feeding rats
           butyl CELLOSOLVE in their diets for three months [1].

           The following effects were observed at the 1.25% dosage level,
           which influenced the study's findings:
           * **Appetite/Diet Consumption:** Diet consumption was significantly
           depressed in both male and female rats [2].
           * **Body Weight:** Body weight gain was depressed to approximately
           one-half that of the control group [2].
           * **Organ Weights:** Mean liver and kidney weights were increased
           [2].
           * **Histopathology:** Testicular atrophy was observed at gross
           autopsy and verified microscopically [8].

           At the 0.25% dosage level, the following effects were observed:
           * **Appetite/Diet Consumption:** Appetite was depressed [8].
           * **Body Weight:** Weight gain was depressed [2]. Statistically
           significant depression in weight gain was noted in males [2].
           * **Histopathology:** Testicular atrophy was observed and confirmed
           microscopically [8].

           The study concluded that the 0.05% level was without deleterious
           effect on the measured criteria [9]. Therefore, the 0.05% level was
           identified as the level without observed adverse effects.

           exposure type (ORAL, DERMAL, or INHALATION) for the toxicology
           study, then classify the exposure method.
EXPECTED : Not applicable
ANSWER   : The provided documents do not identify an exposure type such as
           oral, dermal, or inhalation, as this is an *in vitro* study using
           bacterial strains rather than an *in vivo* study [3, 9!].

           The exposure methods used in the study are the Ames plate
           incorporation and pre-incubation methods [9]. This process involves
           mixing bacterial culture, molten top agar, the test item
           formulation, and S9-mix or phosphate buffer, and then overlaying
           the mixture onto sterile plates of Vogel-Bonner Minimal agar [2].

           mucosa\*\* - gastrointestinal toxicity;<br>- Significant reductions
           in absolute weights of the liver, kidneys, spleen at higher doses
           (LOAEL);<br>- Relative splenic-to-body weight ratios were notably
           lower at 3000 ppm.
ANSWER   : The study is a 13-week subchronic inhalation toxicity study
           involving repeated exposure [1], so it is a repeated dose study.

           The no-observed-effect level (NOEL) for this study was determined
           to be 500 ppm [4]. The following effects observed at higher
           concentrations (1500 and 3000 ppm) influenced the study's findings
           and the determination of the NOEL:

           * **Activity levels:** Animals exposed to 1500 and 3000 ppm
           displayed acute, transient signs of reduced activity levels during
           exposure [7].
           * **Body weight and feed consumption:** Treatments at 1500 and 3000
           ppm produced decreased body weight and feed consumption throughout
           much of the exposure period [3]. While the 500 ppm treatment
           resulted in some reductions in feed consumption, it was not
           accompanied by statistically significant reductions in body weight
           [3].
           * **Respiratory tract irritation:** Signs of upper respiratory
           tract irritation were observed in the nasal passages of animals
           exposed to 1500 and 3000 ppm, including necrosis of the olfactory
           epithelium [7].
           * **Stomach irritation:** Gross necropsy and microscopic
           observations showed signs of irritation to the glandular stomach
           and necrosis in the non-glandular stomach in some 3000 ppm female
           rats [3]. Inflammation of the stomach mucosa was also observed
           microscopically in a few 3000 ppm female rats [7].

           Although changes in organ weights (such as lung-to-body weight
           ratios) were noted, they were considered to reflect reduced caloric
           intake rather than direct test substance-related organ toxicity
           [6]. Similarly, while some serum chemistry values differed from the
           control group, they were not considered toxicologically important
           or indicative of cumulative effects [6].
TARGET   : 1.5 Inhalation_repd.pdf — 10/10 sources from target, best at rank 1

           treatment group, the maximum dose level was limited to 78.1 µg/ml
           due to toxicity [2].

PROMPT   : regarding 1.9_Inhalation_Repd, answer the following: Answer this
           question only if the study is a repeated dose study. Otherwise,
           answer 'not applicable'. What effects on the test animals changed
           the NOAEL or the classification hazard? This could be changes in
           food consumption, organ weight change, weight change, or any other
           health problem observed in the animal due to the substance. If
           there were none, answer "null".
EXPECTED : - Changes in food consumption : Both mid- and high-exposure male
           groups showed reduced food consumption throughout the study
           compared to controls.<br><br>- Weight change (body weight
           reduction) : Mean body weights of mid- and high-exposure males were
           significantly lower than those of control group males by 9.2% and
           13.3%, respectively, at terminal necropsy (study week 13).<br><br>-
           Organ weight change (epididymides atrophy): Mild multifocal atrophy
           in the epididymides observed particularly in males from both
           exposure periods, with more pronounced effects noted by week 13 for
           higher exposures.
ANSWER   : The study described in 1.9_Inhalation_Repd is a repeated dose
           study, as it involved a 13-week subchronic inhalation toxicity
           study with 65 exposures [1].

           Regarding the effects that influenced the determination that a No
           Observable Effect Level (NOEL) could not be established, the
           following observations were noted:

           * **Clinical Signs:** Intermittent whole-body spasms were observed
           in a dose-related manner in all exposed groups during exposure [2].
           Hyperactivity was observed one-hour post-exposure in all test
           groups [2]. Occasional hypoactivity and excessive grooming were
           noted in the high-exposure group [2]. Wet yellow urogenital matting
           and salivation were noted in the high-exposure group [2].
           * **Body Weight and Food Consumption:** Mean body weights were
           decreased in mid- and high-exposure group males, with terminal
           necropsy weights being 9.2% and 13.3% lower than the control group,
           respectively [5]. Mean food consumption was lower in mid- and
           high-exposure group males throughout the study [5].
           * **Hematology:** Decreased platelet and hemoglobin means were
           observed in high-exposure group males and females at study weeks
           three and 13 [5]. Decreased MCH values were noted in high-exposure
           group males at week three and in both sexes at week 13 [5].
           * **Organ Weights and Pathology:** Mean absolute seminal vesicle
           weight, absolute and relative epididymides weights, and mean
           absolute and relative prostate weights were decreased in the
           high-exposure group (and mid-exposure group for prostate weights)
           [4]. Microscopic examination revealed mild multifocal atrophy of
           the testes in one high-exposure group male [5]. Additionally,
           decreased epididymal sperm numbers and motility, along with an
           increased incidence of morphologically abnormal sperm, were
           observed in high-exposure group males at weeks six and 13 [5].

           Because of these data, a NOEL could not be established for THFA via
           whole-body inhalation after 13 weeks of exposure [4].

EXPECTED : Not applicable

           the toxicology study was oral, dermal, or inhalation. The study is
           described as an "in vitro study for the detection of structural
           chromosomal aberrations in cultured mammalian cells" [6].

           Regarding the exposure method, the study involved treating
           duplicate cultures of human lymphocytes with the test item [6]. The
           cultures were incubated at approximately 37°C, 5% CO2 in humidified
           air [6]. The specific exposure conditions included:

           * **4-hour exposure** followed by a 20-hour recovery period in
           treatment-free media [6].
           * **24-hour continuous exposure** [6].

           These exposures were conducted both with and without metabolic
           activation (S9-mix) [9].

PROMPT   : regarding 2.2_Dermal_Repd, answer the following: Answer this
           question only if the study is a repeated dose study. Otherwise,
           answer 'not applicable'. What effects on the test animals changed
           the NOAEL or the classification hazard? This could be changes in
           food consumption, organ weight change, weight change, or any other
           health problem observed in the animal due to the substance. If
           there were none, answer "null".
EXPECTED : CRITICAL EFFECTS : Lower mean body weights and weight gains;
           Decreased spermatogenesis (sperm count, production rate, motility).
           <br>NOAEL DETERMINATION: 100 mg/kg/day for male rats and 300
           mg/kg/day for female rats. <br><br>Critical Effects Influencing
           NOAEL or Classification Hazard:<br><br>- \*\*Lower Mean Body
           Weights and Weight Gains\*\*: Observed at a dose of 1000 mg/kg/day
           in both males and females over the course of 13 weeks, indicating
           systemic toxicity.<br> <br>- \*\*Decreased Spermatogenesis (Sperm
           Count, Production Rate, Motility)\*\*: Noted particularly in male
           rats treated with THFA dermal application. Decreases were observed
           at doses of 300 and 1000 mg/kg/day for sperm count and production
           rate; motility decrease specifically noted at the higher
           dose.<br><br>These effects are pivotal as they directly influenced
           NOAEL determination, highlighting systemic toxicity
           thresholds:<br><br>- \*\*NOAEL Determination\*\*: Identified to be
           100 mg/kg/day for male rats based on decreased spermatogenesis and
           lower body weight metrics. For female rats, it was determined to be
           300 mg/kg/day after considering the adverse outcomes of reduced
           mean body weights.<br>
ANSWER   : The study described in `2.2_Dermal_Repd.pdf` is a repeated dose
           study, as it involved administering the test article (THFA) to rats
           for 13 consecutive weeks [4].

           The following effects were observed in the test animals, which
           influenced the determination of the no observable effect level
           (NOEL) for systemic toxicity:

           * **Body Weight:** Following 13 consecutive weeks of treatment,
           lower mean body weights and body weight gains were observed in both
           males and females at the 1000 mg/kg/day dose level [1].
           * **Spermatogenesis:** An adverse effect on spermatogenesis was
           noted after 13 weeks of administration [1]. Specifically, the mean
           number of sperm in the testis and the mean sperm production rate
           were decreased in males in both the 300 and 1000 mg/kg/day groups
           [1]. Additionally, a decrease in the mean percentage of motile
           sperm was noted in the 1000 mg/kg/day group males [1].

           Based on these data, the NOEL for systemic toxicity was determined
           to be 100 mg/kg/day for males and 300 mg/kg/day for females [3].

           The corpus does not explicitly state that these effects changed a
           "classification hazard," only that they were used to establish the
           NOEL [3!]. Other parameters, such as food consumption, hematology,
           serum chemistry, organ weights, and histopathological lesions in
           reproductive organs, were unaffected by the test article [1].

ANSWER   : The study described in `2.4_Oral_Repd.pdf` is a repeated dose

           rats over a period of several weeks (e.g., postnatal day 22 to

           explicitly state that these findings changed the NOAEL or the
           classification hazard. However, the following effects were noted in
           the study of juvenile/peripubertal female rats:

           * **Body weight:** Initial reduced body weight gain (first two
           days) and mean body weight on PND 24 and 25 (effects were not
           statistically significant and did not persist); reduced body weight
           at the time of vaginal opening [7].
           * **Organ weights:** Increased absolute and relative liver weight;
           increased relative kidney weight; decreased absolute and relative
           pituitary weight; decreased absolute ovarian and uterine weights
           [7].
           * **Clinical/Hormonal/Other:** Decrease in the percentage of
           regularly cycling animals; increased serum TSH and a
           non-statistically significant decrease in serum T4; increased
           thyroid follicular cell height and decreased colloid area;
           increased serum cholesterol, total protein, and globulin; decreased
           serum triglycerides [7].

           For the male rats, the document notes that histopathological
           evaluation of the thyroid, kidney, testis, and epididymis was
           performed, but it does not list specific findings for these organs
           in the provided excerpts [8].

           The documents state that the WBCSD/TIP has not made a determination
           that these results constitute reliable evidence of a significant
           risk of injury to human health or the environment, and therefore,

ANSWER   : The study described in `2.5_inhalation_repd.pdf` is a dose
           range-finding phase of a 90-day inhalation study, which involves
           daily administration of the test substance over fourteen days [1].
           As this is a repeated dose study, the answer is as follows:

           The study reports that test item-related increases in mean relative
           organ weights were noted in the liver, kidney, and adrenal glands
           [1]. Specifically, at 10,000 mg/in³, mean relative liver, kidney,
           and adrenal weights were significantly increased in males, and
           relative liver weight was significantly increased in females [1].
           At 1,000 mg/in³, the only statistically significant effect was
           increased relative adrenal gland weight in males [2]. Additionally,
           the laboratory reported that the animals' tails were stiff and had
           quick, short-lasting movements [2].

           However, the provided text does not explicitly state that these
           effects changed the NOAEL (No-Observed-Adverse-Effect Level) or the
           classification hazard; it notes that because this was only a dose
           range-finding study, histopathologic examination was not performed
           [2!].

           exposure type (ORAL, DERMAL, or INHALATION) for the toxicology
           study, then classify the exposure method.
EXPECTED : In vitro / Not applicable
ANSWER   : The provided documents do not state whether the exposure type for
           the toxicology study was oral, dermal, or inhalation.

           Regarding the exposure method, the study involved an *in vitro*
           mammalian chromosome aberration test [3]. The treatment method
           involved exposing CHO (Chinese Hamster Ovary) cells to the test
           article in a laboratory setting [4!]. Specifically, the cells were
           treated by refeeding flasks with either complete medium (for
           non-activated exposure) or an S9 reaction mixture (for S9-activated
           exposure), to which a dosing solution of the test article or
           solvent was added [10]. The treatment occurred at 37± 1°C in a
           humidified atmosphere of 5± 1% CO2 in air [10].

PROMPT   : regarding 2.8_Dermal_Sens, answer the following: Determine the
           exposure type (ORAL, DERMAL, or INHALATION) for the toxicology
           study, then classify the exposure method.
EXPECTED : Dermal: Topical Application
ANSWER   : The toxicology study for Dihexyl ether is a dermal exposure study
           [5!]. The study objective was to assess the skin sensitization
           potential of the substance following its "dermal application to
           mice" [5!].

           The exposure method is the Local Lymph Node Assay (LLNA) [5, 9!].
           This method involves the application of the test item to the dorsal
           aspect of the ears of mice to measure the proliferation of
           lymphocytes in the auricular lymph nodes draining the site of
           exposure [9!].
